### 3.14 Guardrail: platform-managed, tenant opt-out per agent node / workflow (OD-R, round 3)

**What is already true and stays as is (§2.7 #18).** Guardrail POLICY (`TenantGuardrailPolicy`, the eight `policy-catalogue.ts` checks, `AiRoutingPolicy` `guardrail.*` selection) is managed by the platform admin only (`assertSuperAdmin` on all four availability methods; `SUPER_ADMIN_ONLY_TASK_PREFIXES`), lives in ONE SYSTEM row, is never cloned, and resolves tenant-row-or-SYSTEM in `apps/guardrail`. The per-tenant `TenantGuardrailPolicy` row is KEPT as a **platform-admin instrument** (a super admin narrowing one customer's posture through `PUT admin/guardrail/availability/:tenantId`) — collapsing to SYSTEM-only would remove a capability at no saving. Nothing to build for clauses 1-2.

**The opt-out (clause 3) — one boolean, three authoring places, one precedence, one wire field, one record.**

| Layer | Shape | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| Agent-level default | `AGENT_PARAMETER_SCHEMAS[TEXT_GENERATION].guards` (`agent-schemas.ts:115-120,241`) gains `enabled: { type: 'boolean', default: true }` beside `input` / `output` | L0 (wave 1) |
| Node override | `CORE_AGENT_SCHEMA` (`node-config-schemas.ts:1703`) gains `guardrail: { type: 'object', additionalProperties: false, properties: { enabled: { type: 'boolean' } } }` — ABSENT = inherit (tri-state by absence, the `mcpToolsEnabled` null-widening precedent, `harness-policy.service.ts:154-166`) | L0 |
| Workflow default | `CORE_TRIGGER_SCHEMA` (`:1662`) gains the same `guardrail` object — the workflow-level switch (the trigger is the graph's one mandatory entry node, so it is where a per-workflow fact belongs; `core.trigger` itself stays mandatory and un-disableable) | L0 |
| Precedence | `resolveGuardrailDecision({ node, workflow, agent }) → { enabled: boolean; source: 'node' \| 'workflow' \| 'agent' \| 'default' }` — node > workflow > agent > `true`; pure function in `packages/workflow-contract/src/guardrail-optout.ts` (new) with a Python mirror `apps/harness/src/harness/temporal/interpreter/guardrail_optout.py` (new) held to `tests/contracts/guardrail-optout.fixture.json` (new, 8 cases: every combination of absent / true / false at the three levels that changes the answer) — the CEL / template-grammar parity discipline | L0 |
| Compiled — workflow | `CompiledPolicyBindings.guardrail: { enabled: boolean } \| null` (additive-optional, default `null` = no workflow opinion) on all three sides (`compiler.ts:94-111`, `compiled-config.schema.json:266-`, `compiled_config.py:197-212`) from the trigger's `config.guardrail`; the compiled `core.agent` node keeps its own `config.guardrail` verbatim | L2 (wave 2a) |
| Compiled — agent | `AgentCompiledConfig.guardrail: { enabled: boolean }` (`packages/types/src/agent.ts:91-105`) from `parameters.guards.enabled ?? true`; `ResolvedAgent` exposes it; `GET /internal/agents/resolve` carries it | L8 (wave 2b, `compile` in `agent.service.ts`) + L14 (`agent-resolver.service.ts`) |
| Publish finding | `GUARDRAIL_OPTED_OUT` — WARNING, never blocks — on every `core.agent` node whose resolved decision is `false`, and on the trigger when the workflow default is `false`; emitted by `publishFindings` (L0) so the Studio rail and the agent findings show it | L0 |
| Wire | every TEXT `/api/v1/generate` post carries `guardrail_policy: { enabled: <decision>, require_medical?, include_reasoning? }`; `TextRequestEnrichmentService.applyGuardrailDecision(body, decision)` (new, beside `applyTenantGuardrailPolicy`, `text-request-enrichment.service.ts:177`) merges into the existing block. Producers: `agent-invocation.service.ts` (agent decision), `live-documentation.service.ts` realtime `core.agent` (node > workflow > agent through `resolveGuardrailDecision`), `summary.service.ts` (the resolved agent's decision), harness `nodes/core.py` + `text_generate.py` via a new `guardrail_policy` kwarg on `text_client.generate` (`text_client.py:123-137`), the draft-agent test (§3.8), the prompt test (`prompt-management.service.ts:1339` — always `true`; a prompt test has no node), the playground proxy (unchanged — `applyTenantGuardrailPolicy` already runs, `enabled` absent = platform) | L14 (wave 2b) — the call-site lines in files L3 owns in the same wave (`agent-invocation.service.ts`, `live-documentation.service.ts`, `core.py`) are HANDED to L3 as one-line requests; L14 owns the helper, the harness client, `summary.service.ts`, `text_generate.py` and `apps/text` |
| `apps/text` | `GuardrailPolicyOverride.enabled: bool \| None = None` (`requests.py:106-107`; `None` = no opinion, the block's own rule); `resolve_posture` (`guardrail_posture.py:87-111`) becomes `enabled = tenant.enabled if isinstance(tenant.enabled, bool) else platform.enabled` — **a pushed `false` turns BOTH gates off for that call; a pushed `true` cannot turn a platform-OFF switch on** (the platform kill switch stays the floor: `enabled = platform.enabled and (tenant.enabled if set else True)`); the `external_guardrail_disabled` short-circuit (`external_guardrail.py:74-79,145-146`) gains a distinct reason `tenant_opted_out` so the two are never confused; the three "platform-only" statements (§2.7 #19) are rewritten; the fail posture (no `fail_open`) is untouched | L14 |
| Record | (a) usage: `attributesJson.guardrail: 'opted_out' \| 'screened' \| 'platform_off'` — a new `USAGE_ATTRIBUTE_KEYS` entry (`usage-attributes.ts:46-57`, `'string'`; a DIMENSION: finance and safety both group by it) stamped by every emitter in §3.13 from the decision + the TEXT response's `guardrail` block; (b) audit: the publish `ResourceUpdated` event for an agent / workflow already carries the full `parameters` / `graph` diff — no new event; (c) trajectory: the harness `SKIPPED(disabled_by_config)` shape is reused for the gate — the step result carries `guardrail: "opted_out"`; (d) the TEXT response's existing `guardrail` usage block reports `reason: tenant_opted_out` | L11 (the allow-list key, wave 1) + L14 (stamping) |
| Console | §3.10 rows for L5 (agent switch) and L6 (node tri-state + workflow default + chip) | L5, L6 |
| Boundary — **D-1 ANSWERED YES (2026-09-06)** | The opt-out ALSO covers the mandatory clinical guard nodes: `enabled` stops being withheld from the node types in `MANDATORY_NODE_TYPES` (`node-config-schemas.ts:2226-2247`), the consultation / summarization rule sets keep requiring those nodes to be PRESENT (a disabled node still satisfies presence), and `requires: [\'guard.groundedness\']` (`node-registry.ts:1267,1286,1305`) is satisfied by a present-but-disabled guard. Presence stays mandatory; EXECUTION becomes optional, observable and attributable. Seven change points, three of which are "no code" — §3.14a | L14 (wave 2b) |

What this deliberately does NOT do: no per-tenant "guardrail off" row (a tenant switch would be a fourth tier of the same fact and re-open the "no off" argument TASK-886 settled); no `fail_open`; no change to the policy catalogue or to `apps/guardrail`; no opt-out for a DIRECTION separately (a node is either screened or not — `assertBothDirectionsCovered` stays); and no relaxation of node PRESENCE — after D-1 a mandatory node may be DISABLED, never REMOVED (§3.14a #4), and the two graph boundaries (`core.trigger`, `core.output`) keep their withholding.

#### 3.14a D-1 (owner answer, 2026-09-06): the opt-out extends to the mandatory clinical guard nodes

> **D-1: does the opt-out extend to the seven mandatory clinical guard nodes? — YES.**

The smallest safe change is ONE filter, TWO comments, ONE test expectation and ONE finding case.
Nothing in the rule catalogue, in the `requires` check or in either runtime moves, because none of
them reads `config.enabled` — verified, not assumed (#4, #5 below).

| # | Change point | File (verified on `dev-2.2`) | What changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The withholding filter | `packages/workflow-contract/src/node-config-schemas.ts:2252` (`withRuntimeProperties`) | `!(name === 'enabled' && MANDATORY_NODE_TYPES.has(key))` becomes `!(name === 'enabled' && GRAPH_BOUNDARY_NODE_TYPES.has(key))`, a new two-member set `['core.trigger', 'core.output']`. `MANDATORY_NODE_TYPES` (`:2226-2247`) STAYS exactly as it is — it is the registry mirror the parity test asserts — it simply stops gating the `enabled` fold. Result: `guardrail.check`, `consultation.consentGate`, `consultation.captureBinding`, `consultation.phiHop`, `consultation.persistDraft`, `consultation.finalizeAssurance`, `input.context_binding`, `generate.text`, `output.deliver` and the three `stt.*` types all OFFER `enabled`, `default: true` (ABSENT MEANS ON is unchanged) |
| 2 | The two in-code statements that call this a compliance defect | `node-config-schemas.ts:2205-2224` — the `MANDATORY_NODE_TYPES` docstring, in particular `:2211-2214` (*"On `consultation.consentGate` that is a consent gate a tenant admin can switch off, which is a compliance defect in a healthcare product, not a feature; on `consultation.phiHop` it is a redaction hop that stops redacting"*) — and `:2172-2176`, the `NODE_ENABLED_PROPERTY.description` clause *"and mandatory nodes do not offer it"* | REWRITTEN to record the owner decision rather than argue against it: the `mandatory` class still means "must be PRESENT on every path from `core.start` to a terminal" (which is what `rule-catalogue.ts` enforces and what publish still refuses to let a tenant remove); it no longer means "must EXECUTE". The rewrite keeps the mirror rationale, and names the decision (D-1, TASK-890, 2026-09-06) plus its three compensating controls: the publish WARNING (#6), the per-call ledger attribution (#7), and the graph-boundary exception (#1) |
| 3 | The mirror test | `packages/workflow-contract/src/__tests__/node-enabled-toggle.task852.test.ts:15-25,72-93` | `'a mandatory node type has no disable toggle at all'` flips to `'every node type except the two graph boundaries declares the enabled toggle'`; the registry↔literal-set mirror assertion is KEPT — it is the only thing stopping `MANDATORY_NODE_TYPES` drifting from the registry's `mandatory` class, and D-1 does not weaken that |
| 4 | Mandatory PRESENCE and guard attachment — **no code change** | `packages/workflow-contract/src/rule-catalogue.ts:122-135,273-298,421-514` (the summarization + consultation core rule sets) and `packages/workflow-contract/src/port-validation.ts:302-321` (`requires`) | verified: NO predicate under `packages/workflow-contract/src/predicates/` reads `config.enabled`; `REQUIRED_PATH_THROUGH` and the `NODE_TYPE_PRESENT` rules read the GRAPH, and the `requires` check reads NEIGHBOUR TYPES only. So a present-but-disabled node still satisfies every mandatory-presence rule, and a present-but-disabled `guard.groundedness` already satisfies `requires` on the three `agent.*` generation types. Publish keeps REFUSING a graph in which the node was deleted — the opt-out is a switch, never a deletion |
| 5 | Execution — **no code change** | `packages/applications/src/services/consultation/live-documentation/realtime/realtime-lane.ts:64-65,215` (`enabled: node.config?.enabled !== false`); `apps/harness/src/harness/temporal/interpreter/workflow.py:569` and `core_loop_workflow.py:338` (`NodeActivityResult(status="SKIPPED", reason="disabled_by_config")`) | the observable skip path already exists on BOTH lanes and already carries a reason; a disabled mandatory node is a `SKIPPED` step, never a silent no-op |
| 6 | Publish finding | `packages/workflow-contract/src/publish-findings.ts` (created by L0 in wave 1; L14 adds this case) | `GUARDRAIL_OPTED_OUT` — **WARNING, never blocking** — is emitted once per disabled node and NAMES every disabled mandatory node by `id`, `type` and the registry classes that made it mandatory, alongside the `core.agent` / `core.trigger` cases §3.14 already lists. It renders in the Studio validation rail and in the agent findings, so the omission is visible at authoring time, at publish time and in `validationReport` afterwards |
| 7 | Record | `packages/applications/src/services/usageLedger/usage-attributes.ts:46-57` (the `guardrail` key L11 lands in wave 1) + the run's step results | a run whose graph disabled any mandatory node records `attributesJson.guardrail: 'opted_out'` on the usage row and carries the DISABLED NODE IDS on the step results, so "this consultation ran without its consent gate" is a query, not an inference from the graph JSON |

**TASK-859 invariant 5 is UNTOUCHED.** *"A timeout never approves; the system never signs"* is about
who DECIDES, not about which nodes may be skipped, and nothing in D-1 touches a decision path.
Concretely: `consultation.hitlGate` — the human sign-off — gains NO `enabled` key from change #1,
because it is the one `gate`-classed node type and it sits in `RUNTIME_PROPERTY_EXCLUSIONS`
(`node-config-schemas.ts:2202`), which returns its schema BEFORE the runtime-property fold is
reached; the filter above never sees it. The review proxy still forwards a HUMAN decision and never
decides (§1.2 inv-5), and no timeout branch changes. Giving `hitlGate` a disable toggle would
additionally require `compileGate` to honour it and is deliberately NOT part of D-1's smallest
change — if the owner wants the sign-off gate skippable, that is a separate, owner-signed decision.

**The two graph boundaries stay withheld** (change #1's new set). `core.trigger` / `core.output` are
in `MANDATORY_NODE_TYPES` for a STRUCTURAL reason, not a clinical one: a disabled entry is a graph
that cannot start and a disabled exit is a graph that cannot deliver. That is an unrunnable graph
rather than a guardrail opinion, so withholding the key there costs nothing and saves D-1 from
inventing a new blocking finding and new runtime semantics for a boundary that is off.

#### 3.14b D-2 (owner answer, 2026-09-06): the external-guardrail switch ships ON

> **D-2: seed the external-guardrail switch on? — YES.**

| Piece | Decision |
|---|---|
| The row | ONE SYSTEM `GlobalSetting` row, `key = 'text.externalGuardrail.enabled'`, `value = true`, `tenantId = SYSTEM_TENANT_ID`, added to the "Platform-owned settings (SYSTEM_TENANT_ID)" block of `packages/database/src/prisma/db_main/seed/11-global-setting.ts:463-475`, create-only like every platform row (a re-seed never reverts a deliberate flip). It is NOT a descriptor default change: the key keeps `maxScope: 'system'`, `globalOnly: true`, `killSwitch: true`, `failMode: 'open-to-default'` (`text-provider-connections.descriptors.ts:78,91-99,164-171`), so a platform admin can still turn the gate off without a deploy |
| Owner / wave | **L14, wave 2b** — the lane that makes the switch mean something. `seed/11-global-setting.ts` is added to §4.3 with L14 as its single owner |
| Precondition 1 — the proof | **proof #8 (§4.4) runs FIRST**, on dev, on the test DB and on the k3s `hope-v2-dev` DB, and its result is recorded in §9. An existing SYSTEM row is left untouched by the create-only seed; a `true` row on a CUSTOMER tenant is a `globalOnly` violation to investigate BEFORE seeding, not after |
| Precondition 2 — the stack | **every stack the e2e suite runs against MUST have a reachable guardrail client, or every generation 503s** — that is fail-closed by design (`generate.py:207-213`, `output_gate.py:202-204`), so turning the switch on without it turns the whole e2e suite red for a reason that is not a bug. What must be true: `apps/guardrail` is running and reachable from `apps/text` at the resolved `GUARDRAIL_URL` in (a) the local dev stack — `pnpm stack:dev` already starts it; (b) **the isolated test stack the API e2e suite runs against — `tests/docker-compose.test.yml` + `pnpm setup:test` / `pnpm test:up:api` — which today starts data infrastructure only, so it must either gain a guardrail service or the e2e run must point TEXT at a running guardrail**; (c) the k3s `hope-v2-dev` namespace — it already runs it. **Who checks: the ORCHESTRATOR, in the primary checkout, BEFORE wave 2b's e2e run** (§4.7 step 5) — never a lane, because the test compose and the `pnpm test:up:*` / `setup:test` scripts are shared surfaces (rule 14 §3) |
| Order | the seed row merges with L14; the orchestrator then runs `pnpm db:seed` (dev) + `pnpm test:db:seed` (test) and the `task-890-guardrail-optout` spec. If precondition 2 cannot be met inside the wave, the seed row is HELD as a one-row follow-up commit and the opt-out still ships — the toggle is correct either way (§9) |
| Cost, recorded | one guardrail round-trip on input plus one delegated `classify` and one PII-span call on output, per generation. The latency / cost delta is measured on the first e2e run with the switch on and recorded in §9 (Risk 22) |


### 3.15 Platform settings: platform-admin-only, global-wide effective (OD-P, round 3)

| Piece | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|
| The guard | `private assertPlatformTierWrite(targetTenantId)`: `if (targetTenantId === SYSTEM_TENANT_ID && !isSuperAdmin(this.requestUser)) throw new ForbiddenException('Platform-wide settings are managed by super administrators only.')` — called in `create` (on `effectiveTenantId`, `:82`), `update` (on the loaded row's `tenantId`, BEFORE the `locked` check — existence first, privilege second, per rule 05), `deleteById` (load the row first — today it soft-deletes blind at `:311`; the load is what makes a foreign id 404 and a SYSTEM id 403 in that order), and the revive-on-create branch (`:95-104`). Identical in shape to `assertMayWriteAtScope` (`settings-registry-write.service.ts:336-340`) and `assertWriteAllowed` (`ai-provider-connection.service.ts:788-796`) | `packages/applications/src/services/globalSetting/globalSetting.service.ts` — L1 |
| Tests | one unit test per method in `globalSetting.service.test.ts` (tenant admin + SYSTEM target → 403; super admin → passes; tenant admin + own tenant → passes; foreign id → 404 before any 403); controller test unchanged | L1 |
| E2E depth | `apps/api/tests/e2e/task-890-platform-settings-gate.spec.ts` (new): tenant-admin JWT `PATCH admin/settings/:id` on a seeded SYSTEM row → 403; super-admin → 200; tenant-admin on another tenant's row → 404; `POST admin/settings` with `tenantId: SYSTEM` from a tenant admin → 403 (today: a raw 500/400 from the interceptor or the extension) | L1 |
| "Global-wide effective" | already true — `AppSettingsService` admits SYSTEM only (`appSettings.service.ts:19-49`), invalidates on `app-settings:invalidate`; retiring `provisionTenantConfigs` (L13) removes the frozen-clone hazard, not a cache defect | nothing to build |
| Console | `/settings` and `/settings-registry` stay tier 20-29; the SYSTEM rows render read-only for a tenant admin from the 403 (a disabled Save with the reason), no nav change | L5 does not touch it; a one-line copy change in `features/settings` is folded into L4's console pass only if the 403 is not already rendered — verify at wave 1 |
| Rule 05 | one row in §Imperative Privilege Checks: "**Platform-tier row of a tenant-manageable resource** — `GlobalSettingService.assertPlatformTierWrite` (with `assertMayWriteAtScope`, `assertWriteAllowed` as the siblings): a SYSTEM-tenant row is super-admin-only (403), a tenant's own row devolves to `manage:GlobalSetting`" | orchestrator, with L1's merge (§8) |

## 4. Implementation Plan

### 4.0 Rules every lane follows (rule 14 brief essentials)

| Element | Value for every lane |
|---|---|
| Ticket / target branch | TASK-890 / `dev-2.2` |
| Worktree | `git worktree add ../hope-v2-task-890-l<n> -b task-890-l<n>` from the CURRENT `dev-2.2` head at spawn time (the orchestrator commits before spawning); a fresh tree needs `pnpm install` (orchestrator runs it) and copies of `.env.dev` / `.env.test` |
| Rules to read | rule 00, 01, 14 always; plus the per-lane list below |
| Must NOT touch | files outside the lane's ownership column; `pnpm install`; `db:push` / `db:migrate` / `test:db:reset` / `db:seed` against the dev or test DB (the shadow-DB recipe in L2 is the only sanctioned DB command); Docker; merges; `git stash`; the five API artifacts (`route-manifest.json`, `openapi.json`, the portal, `vox-node/src/resources/admin/**`) — orchestrator-only; `.claude/rules/*` (the §8 amendments are the orchestrator's, in the primary checkout) |
| TDD | failing test first; RED output pasted; test placement per rule 01 (`__tests__/*.test.ts` colocated; `tests/contracts/` for cross-language fixtures; Python beside the existing suites) |
| Boot smoke | a lane that adds or changes a controller (L1, L7, L8, L10, L11, L12, L13, L14) proves the gateway BOOTS: `pnpm api:build` then `NODE_ENV=test node apps/api/dist/main` until `GET /api/v1/health` answers, and pastes the line (TASK-870 rule; the boot audits `auditAdminRoutePermissions`, `auditAdminControllersDeclareNoApiKeyScopes`, `auditServiceAccountSurface` run only at startup) |
| Python provenance | Python lanes (L0, L2, L3, L10, L14) run `pnpm harness:test` / `pnpm text:test` from the worktree and paste the `assert_source_tree` guard line (`apps/harness/src/harness/tests/conftest.py`; `apps/text` has the `pythonpath` half only — rule 14 §4). **`packages/py-workflow-contract` has NEITHER half** (no `pythonpath` entry, no conftest guard), so from a worktree its pytest must be run as `PYTHONPATH=<tree>/packages/py-workflow-contract/src <conda> python -m pytest` or it silently tests the PRIMARY checkout's copy; from the primary checkout it needs nothing. Its conda invocation is `/Users/taphuynh/miniconda3/bin/conda run -n arcaenv …` — the shell function `conda` is not callable from the agent's non-interactive zsh |
| Return contract | the final message is DATA for the orchestrator: branch, files changed, each gate's command + pasted output, RED evidence per test, deviations from this README, open questions, and the list of requests handed to another lane |
| Fan-out cap | at most FIVE concurrent lanes per wave (rule 14 §2 "cap the fan-out at what you can actually review"); the wave table in §4.2 respects it |

### 4.2 Wave sequencing

```
Wave 1  (5 concurrent)        L0 contract+wiring+guardrail shapes   L1 catalogue+ability+readers+settings guard+H-6 runtime   L7 review proxy+SDK   L11 metering parity (+guardrail key)   L12 readiness
        orchestrator          merge → five artifacts → boot smoke → pnpm db:seed (policy) → data proofs #1,#2,#3,#8 → rule-05 row (§8) → catalogue×readiness join test
Wave 2a (alone)               L2 schema+migration+trio+snapshot(+policyBindings.guardrail)+bridge seed
        orchestrator          merge L2 → pnpm db:push (dev) → pnpm test:db:reset → data proof #4 (zero single-brace rows after seed) → gen:*:check
Wave 2b (5 concurrent — cap)  L3 renderers+realtime metering   L4 typed variables+seeds+picker+metered test   L8 agent service+draft test+compile stamp+H-6 clone   L10 BYO models+slug guard   L14 guardrail opt-out
        orchestrator          merge → five artifacts → boot smoke → pnpm db:seed → data proofs #5,#10,#12 → e2e task-890-*
Wave 3  (5 concurrent — cap)  L5 agents console   L6 studio console   L9 vox admin-surface removal   L13 reference set + retirements + THE FLIP (step v merged LAST)
        orchestrator          merge L13 steps i–iv + L5/L6/L9 → backfill scripts (dev) → test:db:reset → proofs #6,#7,#9,#11 → merge L13 step v → FULL e2e → rules 00/09 amendments (§8) → console build/lint/test → console e2e → README §9
Release +1                    promote PROMPT_VARIABLE_UNDECLARED to ERROR (one constant) — same ticket, second landing (OD-C)
```

Wave 3 lists four lanes plus L13 counted once: L13 delivers TWO merges (steps i–iv, then step v after the backfill proofs) — the fan-out stays at the cap because it is one worktree.

Dependencies: L2 needs L0's `PublishContext` slots and L1's "no reader remains" proof; L3 needs L0's renderer + `resolveGuardrailDecision`, L2's snapshot and L11's helper; L4 needs L0's `templateReferences`, L1's catalogue and L11's helper; L8 needs L1's constants, L2's `resolveReference` and L11's helper; L10 needs L1's `providerClassOf` and L2's columns; **L14 needs L0's shapes + precedence, L2's `policyBindings.guardrail`, L11's `guardrail` key, and L1's `agent-resolver.service.ts` edit merged**; L5/L6 need L1 (catalogue), L4 (shared picker + hooks), L8 (draft test route), L10 (BYO providers in the picker), L12 (readiness in the picker), L7 (`describe` fields for the publish dialog) and L0's guardrail schemas (the toggles); L9 needs L7 merged (shared files); **L13 needs L2 (provenance), L4 (prompt clone), L8 (agent entry point + clone H-6), L1 (runtime H-6), L14 (`ResolvedAgent.guardrail` on the resolve route it edits), and its step v needs proof #9 = 0 after the backfill**.

### 4.6 Day-1 minimum cut vs full cut (re-cut in round 3)

Blockers on the day-1 path: the seven of §2.3 **plus OD-M's flip**, which is not a blocker of the journey (a tenant admin's journey works before the flip — SYSTEM still answers) but is the owner rule's definition of done and is therefore ON the day-1 path as its LAST step, after the backfill: flipping early breaks every unprovisioned tenant with no observable error (§2.7 #22), flipping last costs nothing.

| | Day-1 minimum (the seven BLOCKERs + the OD-M flip, coherent) | Full cut (adds) |
|---|---|---|
| L0 | all of it (incl. the guardrail shapes + precedence — L14 needs them) | — |
| L1 | all of it (the reader switch is a precondition for L2's drop; the settings guard and the H-6 runtime fix are three lines each) | — |
| L2 | all of it | — |
| L3 | flavours 3-6 through the shared renderer; flavours 1-2 deleted; the trigger `resolved` read; the realtime lane metered; the three handed guardrail lines | — |
| L11 | the invocation route (blocking + SSE) precheck + record; the helper; `trigger` + `guardrail` keys | the resolve-route precheck, STT/NER/embed prechecks, proxy precheck, `trigger` on existing emitters |
| L7 | the review proxy (gateway + `hope.workflows.reviews.*`) | `hope.workflows.schema`, the summary fields, `useAgentInvocation` (OD-F), `signWebhookTrigger`, `modesFor` socket |
| L8 | the context pin + findings; `providerClassOf` + `availability` read; the compile stamp; the H-6 clone fix | the draft test route; the seed binding form |
| L4 | converted seeds + array declarations (L3's deletion needs them); `useModelCatalogue` + the shared picker (L5 needs both); the prompt test-run metered | list projection, test-run variables form, "derive from content", the banner |
| L14 | `apps/text` + the harness client + the enrichment helper + the agent-invocation and durable-lane decisions + the ledger stamp (the OD-R MUST: "tenant admin MUST be able to opt out"); **the D-1 `enabled` filter + the `GUARDRAIL_OPTED_OUT` mandatory-node case (§3.14a #1, #6) — it is the owner's answer to "each agent node or workflow" and it is three edited lines plus a flipped test; the D-2 seed row (§3.14b) once proof #8 and the stack precondition are green** | the realtime-lane decision, the `summary.service.ts` decision, the proxy stamp, the comment rewrites (incl. the two D-1 statements) |
| L13 | ALL five steps: provision every kind + backfill, reference reads, the fail-closed resolve + the two resolvers + the prompt clone read, the pinned tests, the flip; the two clone retirements (OD-O, OD-P — decided) | — (the re-sync route's `refresh-locked` mode can ship `missing-only`-first if time is short) |
| L5 | Provider → Model over the catalogue (the current call is a 403 for a tenant admin); the context-schema picker; the shared picker in the wizard; the `guards.enabled` switch; drop `includeTemplates` + re-key the badge (the flip needs it) | editable draft fields, version pin + bindings UI, draft test panel, publish dialog, `?create=1`, the readiness badge |
| L6 | the two guardrail fields in the inspector (OD-R names "each agent node or workflow" — the node/workflow toggles ARE the requirement) | the rest of it |
| L10 | — (BYO models are OD-A's full answer, not a blocker: today's single-model connections keep working; the slug guard ships with it) | all of it |
| L12 | — (readiness `unknown` is honest; OD-L's monitoring is a full-cut MUST) | all of it |
| L9 | — | all of it (OD-F's removal; must land before the SDK release that ships `useAgentInvocation`, so `publish-sdk` waits for it) |

The day-1 cut = L0 + L1 + L2 + L3 + L11 (subset) + L7 (reviews) + L8 (subset) + L4 (subset) + L14 (subset) + L13 (all) + L5 (subset) + L6 (the two fields) — four waves stay four (wave 3 = L5 subset + L6 subset + L13). Deferrable without breaking the journey: L9, L10, L12 and every "full cut (adds)" cell.

### 4.7 Orchestrator merge protocol (per wave, primary checkout)

1. Ask the owner to confirm the merge target (`dev-2.2`), merge each lane branch, resolve conflicts here, never in a worktree.
2. Regenerate the five artifacts when a route changed: `pnpm api:build && pnpm api:route-manifest && pnpm api:openapi && pnpm api:portal && pnpm --filter @arcaai/vox-node gen:admin`, then `pnpm api:openapi:check && pnpm api:portal:check && pnpm --filter @arcaai/vox-node gen:admin:check`.
3. Boot smoke (`GET /api/v1/health` line pasted into §9).
4. DB: after wave 2a `pnpm db:push` (dev) then `pnpm test:db:reset`; after any seed change `pnpm db:seed` (dev) and `pnpm test:db:seed` (test); after L13's step-i merge the two L13 scripts (dev) then `pnpm test:db:reset` (test), then proofs #9 / #10 / #11 — and ONLY then merge L13 step v.
5. Re-run the merged lanes' gates plus `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck:all`, `pnpm test:unit`; the §4.4 proof for that wave; e2e `task-890-*` from wave 2b on; the FULL e2e after L13 step v. **In wave 2b, BEFORE the e2e run: proof #8 and the D-2 stack precondition (§3.14b) — every stack the suite runs against has a reachable guardrail client, or L14's seed row is held.**
6. **Close the wave with the `opus` wave-close agent (§4.9)**: it re-runs the wave's gates itself on the merged tree, reviews the diff against §4.1 / §4.3, fixes what it can, names what it cannot, and returns an explicit "closed / not closed" verdict. A "not closed" verdict blocks the next wave's spawns.
7. Remove a worktree only after its merge commit exists on `dev-2.2` (rule 14 §5) — L13's worktree stays until step v is merged.
8. TASK-870's README gets one Change History row when L12 lands (registry count 209 → 214).
9. Rule amendments (§8) are the orchestrator's edits in the primary checkout, in the SAME merge commit as the lane that makes them true: rule 05 row with L1 (wave 1); rule 00 §Configuration Principles paragraph + rule 09 §Tenant-first one-liner with L13 step v (wave 3).

### 4.8 Fast-win path — critical path, estimates, what the owner sees after each wave

Assumptions: one `opus` agent (or one engineer) per lane, gates and evidence INCLUDED in the estimate, no rework loop, orchestrator merge + artifacts + proofs ≈ 0.5 elapsed day per wave (1 day for wave 3, which has two L13 merges and the backfill); lanes inside a wave run concurrently, so the wave's elapsed time is its LONGEST lane; the day-1 subsets of §4.6 are what is estimated, the full-cut remainder is listed separately.

| Wave | Lanes on the day-1 path (engineer-days) | Elapsed (longest + orchestrator) | Critical-path lane |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | L0 3.5 · L1 3.5 · L11 (subset) 1.5 · L7 (reviews) 1.5 | 3.5 + 0.5 = **4** | L0 / L1 (tie) |
| 2a | L2 3 | 3 + 0.5 = **3.5** | L2 |
| 2b | L3 3 · L14 (subset) 2 · L8 (subset) 1.5 · L4 (subset) 2 | 3 + 0.5 = **3.5** | L3 |
| 3 | L13 4 (steps i–iv ≈ 3, step v ≈ 1 incl. the full e2e) · L5 (subset) 2 · L6 (two fields) 0.5 | 4 + 1 = **5** | L13 |
| **Total** | **≈ 28.5 engineer-days** | **≈ 16 elapsed working days** | L0/L1 → L2 → L3 → L13 |

Full cut adds ≈ 19 engineer-days off the critical path: L12 3, L10 4, L9 1.5, L6 remainder 2.5, L5 remainder 2, L11 remainder 1, L7 remainder 1.5, L4 remainder 1.5, L8 remainder 1.5, L14 remainder 0.5 — each fits its wave without lengthening it EXCEPT L10 (4 d in wave 2b, longer than L3), which would move wave 2b to 4.5 elapsed; so L10 is the first to defer if the calendar matters.

What the owner can see working after each wave (one demo each):

| After | Demo (tenant-admin JWT unless stated) |
|---|---|
| Wave 1 | `GET /admin/ai-models/catalogue?taskType=TEXT_GENERATION` answers the two-group picker (today: 403); publishing a graph whose `core.agent` has `config: {}` is refused with `AGENT_REF_MISSING`; `PATCH admin/settings/:id` on a SYSTEM row → 403; every `POST /agents/:slug/invocations` writes a ledger row with `trigger: AGENT_INVOCATION`; a `core.humanReview` node can be decided through `hope.workflows.reviews.decide` |
| Wave 2a | `pnpm db:push` + `db:seed` leave the DB with the new columns, `wireModelId` backfilled, a SYSTEM `consultation-legacy-v1` schema, and zero single-brace prompt rows (proof #4) — a data milestone, no UI |
| Wave 2b | one prompt with `{{context.patientAge}}` renders the SAME text on the realtime and the durable lane (today: literal on one of them); a draft agent test dry-runs and shows the assembled prompt; the prompt test panel sends typed variables and meters the run; an agent published with `guards.enabled: false` invokes without the guardrail round-trip and the ledger row says `guardrail: opted_out` |
| Wave 3 (steps i–iv) | `POST admin/tenants` (super admin) creates a tenant that OWNS its three platform agents, their assignments and fallback chains, the golden prompts, the workflows and the legacy schema — `Platform` badges read "from platform" on the tenant's own rows; the Studio inspector shows the guardrail tri-state per `core.agent` and the workflow default on the trigger; the console model step lists BYO connections first, then "Hope provider" |
| Wave 3 (step v) | with the flip merged, deleting a tenant's TEXT_GENERATION assignment makes its next invocation fail with `AGENT_NOT_ASSIGNED` naming the task and tenant — and a `POST …/reference-set/sync` puts it back; `grep "'Agent'\|'AgentAssignment'\|'PromptTemplate'\|'PromptVersion'" tenant-scope.ts` inside `SYSTEM_SHARED_READ_MODELS` → none |

Both §6.3 items are now ANSWERED and both land inside L14's existing wave-2b slot, so the critical path is unchanged: **D-1** (YES) is one filter line, a two-member set, two comment rewrites, one flipped test and one finding case (§3.14a) — ≈ 0.5 engineer-day inside L14's 2 d subset, and it touches no lane on the critical path; **D-2** (YES) is one create-only seed row (`seed/11-global-setting.ts`) gated by proof #8 and by the orchestrator's stack check (§3.14b). If the test stack cannot serve a guardrail client in that wave, the row is held as a one-row follow-up commit — the opt-out ships either way, and only the "is the gate live" demo slips.

### 4.9 Execution protocol (owner directive, 2026-09-06)

> **"One team of agents in parallel worktrees, merged into `dev-2.2` wave by wave, with an `opus`
> gated verification / review / bug-fix pass before each wave closes."**

This is HOW §4.1–§4.8 are executed. It adds nothing to the design; it binds the discipline of
`.claude/rules/14-multi-agent-worktrees.md` to this ticket's fourteen lanes.

| Element | Rule for TASK-890 |
|---|---|
| One team, many lanes | ONE team of agents per wave, at most FIVE concurrent (§4.0 fan-out cap, rule 14 §2). Each agent owns exactly one lane for the whole wave; a lane is never split across two agents |
| One worktree per writer | every lane gets its OWN worktree and branch: `git worktree add ../hope-v2-task-890-l<n> -b task-890-l<n>`, branched from the CURRENT `dev-2.2` head at spawn time (the orchestrator commits first, so every tree starts from a base containing the previous wave). Two agents NEVER share a checkout (rule 14 §3) |
| Disjoint file sets | §4.3 governs, and it is the contract: an agent that needs a line in another lane's file HANDS the request to that lane (the L14→L3 one-liners are the worked example) instead of editing it. If two lanes would touch one file, they are one lane or two waves — never two concurrent writers |
| The lane brief | each agent is briefed SELF-CONTAINED (rule 14 §2): a common preamble identical across every lane, plus that lane's §4.1 row verbatim (Goal, Why-this-wave, Tier/effort, Rules to read, Owns, Must-not-touch, TDD list, Steps, Gates, Hands-to) and §4.0's rules-every-lane-follows table. The agent has NOT read this conversation and inherits none of the orchestrator's rules |
| Prompt-cache hygiene | the brief PREAMBLE is byte-identical across lanes and placed FIRST (ticket, branch, §4.0 table, rule list, return contract), with the lane-specific part last, so the shared prefix stays cacheable across the wave's spawns. A follow-up or correction goes to the SAME agent as a message (its context, its worktree), never as a fresh spawn of the same lane — re-spawning discards the cache AND the lane's state |
| Tier per lane | as declared in each §4.1 row (`opus`/high for the deciding lanes L0, L2, L13, L14; cheaper tiers only where the row says so). Escalate a lane on EVIDENCE (a hedged or self-contradicting return), never pre-emptively (rule 14 §1) |
| Shared surfaces are the orchestrator's | merges, branch switches, `pnpm install`, `db:push` / `db:migrate` / `db:seed` / `test:db:reset`, Docker and the test stack, the five API artifacts, `.claude/rules/*`, and the §3.14b guardrail-stack check. No lane runs any of them (§4.0 "Must NOT touch") |
| Wave close — the gate | a wave CLOSES only when ALL of the following have happened, in order: (1) every lane of the wave is merged into `dev-2.2` from the PRIMARY checkout, conflicts resolved there; (2) the orchestrator regenerates the five artifacts and runs the three `:check`s; (3) the wave's gates re-run AFTER the merge (a clean merge is not a passing build) plus `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck:all`, `pnpm test:unit`, and the wave's e2e; (4) the wave's §4.4 data proofs run and their output is pasted into §9; (5) **an `opus` wave-close agent verifies, validates, reviews and bug-fixes the MERGED implementation** |
| The wave-close agent | `opus`, high effort, run in the PRIMARY checkout with NO worktree — at that point it is the only writer, every lane worktree is quiet, and the thing under review is the merged tree, not a branch. Its brief: re-run the wave's gates itself rather than trusting the lanes' pasted output (rule 14 §2 "re-verify the artifacts yourself"), read the merged diff against the wave's §4.1 rows and §4.3 ownership, check the invariants of §1.2 and §1.5, and FIX what it finds in place (small bug-fixes) or name it as a lane request for the next wave (anything structural). It returns: gates re-run with pasted output, proof results, defects found and fixed, defects deferred with a named owner lane, and an explicit "wave N closed / not closed" verdict. A "not closed" verdict blocks the next wave's spawns |
| Reviewer independence | the wave-close agent is NEVER one of the wave's lane agents (a lane cannot certify itself), and its findings are DATA for the orchestrator — a subagent's output never authorises the next step (rule 14 §2) |
| Worktree lifecycle | a worktree is removed ONLY after its merge commit exists on `dev-2.2` and the wave-close agent has passed the wave (rule 14 §5). L13's worktree survives until its step-v merge. `git worktree prune` / `remove --force` are never used to tidy up |
| Wave-specific additions | wave 2a runs alone (L2 owns the migration; the orchestrator runs the shadow-DB recipe's dev-side sync). Wave 2b's close ALSO confirms §3.14b's stack precondition before the e2e run. Wave 3's close happens TWICE: once for L13 steps i–iv + L5/L6/L9, and once for L13 step v after proofs #9/#10/#11, with the FULL e2e |
| Reporting | per wave the orchestrator records, in §9: each lane's branch, its merge commit, the gates with actual output, the proofs, the wave-close agent's verdict, and whether each worktree was removed. An un-merged lane is reported at the TOP of the report, never buried (rule 14 §6) |

## 5. Decisions taken by this plan (owner may override)

| # | Decision | Alternative rejected |
|---|---|---|
| P-1 | Provider → model is a PICKER over two groups (the tenant's BYO connections, then one Hope provider); the agent keeps `modelId` alone (D-2 preserved); `usable` is computed server-side from the existing connection cascade. | A stored `provider` / `providerConnectionId` on `Agent` — a second place for the cascade to diverge; re-opens funding stamping. |
| P-2 | A new read-only route + picker DTO + `read:AiModel`; registry writes unchanged. | Widening `/admin/ai-models` to `read` — the write controller would serve `ModelResponse`'s storage topology and operator identity to tenants. |
| P-3 | One hand-rolled grammar (`{{ path \| default("…") }}`, `{{{{` escape), values are data, TS + Python mirrors, one fixture — the CEL precedent (OD-D: ≈ 3 engineer-days). | Jinja2 / nunjucks — not semantically identical across languages (the fixture would certify a lie), an SSTI-class surface in tenant-authored clinical prompts, a new dependency where `apps/harness` renders with `re.sub` today. |
| P-4 | Unresolved reference: WARNING at publish in release 1 (+ console banner from proof #5), ERROR in release +1; a named error at render from day one (OD-C). | ERROR immediately — makes existing content unpublishable in the same release as the grammar change (Risk 1). |
| P-5 | **(replaces round 1)** NO `PromptVersion.syntax`, no dispatch, no shim, no convert assistant: the single-brace renderers are deleted and the seeds converted (OD-K); proof #4 asserts no single-brace row survives the seed. | The syntax column + one-time classification + convert route — a dual-homed grammar in a pre-production system with no customer content. |
| P-6 | Context schema binds by reference (governed) AND inline (ad hoc) (OD-G); the gateway freezes the DERIVED payload JSON Schema into the compiled artifact; the harness validates the snapshot. | The harness re-reading the row (needs a DB, violates invariant 4); rewriting the reference into `inline` at compile (loses provenance). |
| P-7 | **(replaces round 1)** Context schemas are TENANT-OWNED and stay OUT of `SYSTEM_SHARED_READ_MODELS`; SYSTEM's `consultation-legacy-v1` (and every SYSTEM schema / agent / workflow / prompt) reaches a tenant by CLONE through `TenantReferenceSetService` at creation, re-sync and backfill (OD-H, OD-J). | Shared read of the bridge row (round 1) — cross-tenant read of platform content the owner ruled out; a runtime SYSTEM tier in `WorkflowAssignmentService.resolve` (TASK-896) — a second resolution posture for the same content class. |
| P-8 | One publish contract: `publishFindings(): WorkflowFinding[]` with a `code`, recorded on `validationReport`, blocking on ERROR — the agent side's idiom applied to graphs; enforcement scoped to `core.*` in release 1; `workflowPublishProblems` DELETED and its five callers repointed (OD-K). | A third finding shape; a delegating alias kept "for the seed callers". |
| P-9 | Immutability is console-only (F-6): the edit form gains the fields the API already accepts; `task` and `slug` stay immutable; PUBLISHED rows keep "new version". | A new endpoint or schema change — nothing is missing server-side. |
| P-10 | **(reversed)** The draft test is the prompt test's two-call shape on the admin plane, `dryRun` default; a non-dry run is quota-checked (`monthlyLlmTokens`, no-increment) and metered (`generate.stream`, `trigger: AGENT_TEST`), funding DERIVED (OD-E). | "Not counted against quota" (round 1) — an unmetered loop; a `funding: 'test'` tier (stamping). |
| P-11 | The SDK webhook helper is `signWebhookTrigger` (inbound trigger, `"<timestamp>.<rawBody>"`, `X-Hope-Signature`), added beside the EXISTING `verifyWebhookSignature` (outbound deliveries). | Naming it `verifyWebhookSignature` as the evaluation did — that export already exists for a different contract. |
| P-12 | `Agent.instruction.variables` is `{ [name]: { value } \| { path } }` ONLY; the flat string is rejected and the seed converted (OD-K). | Accepting + deprecating the flat form (round 1). |
| P-13 | Typed prompt variables are an ARRAY (`PromptVariableDeclarationDto[]`), the column stays `Json?`, the seeds' legacy map is converted — no read-side normaliser (OD-K). | A new table — declarations are per version and travel with the snapshot; a normaliser for a shape that only the seeds ever wrote. |
| P-14 | Wave 2 is split: L2 alone (2a), then L3 / L4 / L8 / L10 (2b); wave 1 carries five lanes (the fan-out cap), wave 3 four. | Concurrent L2/L3/L4/L8/L10 — four lanes would edit or depend on the same schema and service files. |

| P-17 | The four dropped generation parameters stay in the schemas (capability-gated) and are plumbed by TASK-891 (OD-I, approved). | Removing them — silently narrows what tenants may tune. |
| P-18 | **(new)** A BYO connection declares its models and each declared model IS a tenant-owned `AiModel` row with `sourceConnectionId` (one table, one fact); D-2, the FK, derived funding and REQ-5 all hold; the SYSTEM registry never sees a tenant write (OD-A). | (b) tenant-writable `AiRoutingPolicy` rows — 13 super-admin gates, the agent path never consults it for `TEXT_GENERATION`, TASK-881 made it the non-agent task-default table; (c) `extraJson.models[]` with no row — breaks `Agent.modelId`'s FK and folds a list into every `provider_overrides` payload; a child `AiProviderConnectionModel` table PLUS mirror rows — two sources for one fact. |

| P-24 | Guardrail policy is NOT part of the reference set (its resolver widens on absence by design, `tenant-scope.ts:178-190`) — OD-R confirmed in round 3; the per-tenant row stays a platform-admin instrument. | Cloning it — every tenant would acquire an opinion it never expressed; collapsing to SYSTEM-only — removes a capability at no saving. |
| P-25 | `provisionTenantModelCatalog` and `provisionTenantConfigs` are deleted by L13 (OD-O / OD-P decided in round 3); plan-tier gating becomes a catalogue FILTER; the runtime cascade answers settings on absence. | Repointing the settings clone to SYSTEM — still a clone that never sees platform updates (the same defect as the catalogue clone). |

H-6 is fixed by CLS context in both readers, not by widening `AgentModelFallback`. | Flipping in wave 1 or 2 — crashes `AgentAssignmentService.resolve` and silently empties every `Agent` visible read for unprovisioned tenants (§2.7 #22); keeping the runtime SYSTEM tier — contradicts OD-M; widening `AgentModelFallback` — a third shared-read content model on the day the rule says zero. |
| P-27 | **(round 3)** Guardrail opt-out = ONE boolean declared on three existing JSON shapes (agent `guards.enabled`, `core.agent.config.guardrail`, `core.trigger.config.guardrail`), precedence node > workflow > agent > `true` in one pure function with a TS/Python parity fixture, compiled into `compiledConfig.guardrail` / `policyBindings.guardrail`, carried as `guardrail_policy.enabled` on every TEXT post, honoured in `resolve_posture` with the platform switch as the FLOOR (a tenant can turn OFF, never ON), recorded as `attributesJson.guardrail` and a distinct `tenant_opted_out` reason. **Amended by D-1 (P-32): the mandatory clinical guard nodes are IN scope — their PRESENCE stays mandatory, their EXECUTION becomes optional.** | A tenant-writable `TenantGuardrailPolicy` (per tenant, not per node; cannot express "off" by construction — `assertBothDirectionsCovered`); a new column; a per-direction opt-out; a `fail_open`. |
| P-28 | **(round 3)** The legacy `GlobalSettingService` gets the same imperative `targetTenantId === SYSTEM && !isSuperAdmin → 403` guard its two siblings carry, existence resolved before privilege; landed in L1 (wave 1) with a unit test per method and one e2e depth spec; rule 05 gains a row. | Relying on the emergent protection (interceptor + Prisma write pinning) — it throws raw errors, is not auditable, and depends on "only a super admin can ever hold CLS = SYSTEM" staying true across every future entry point; evaluating the CASL condition at the route — a new mechanism where a three-line precedent exists. |
| P-29 | **(round 3)** A BYO model whose generated slug equals a SYSTEM slug is REFUSED at declaration (409 naming the SYSTEM id + the `byo-<provider>-…` suggestion). | Auto-prefixing silently — hides the collision from the admin who typed the id; allowing it — the tenant's row shadows the Hope row for every by-slug resolver in that tenant (§2.7 #25). |
| P-30 | **(round 3)** `AgentAssignmentService.resolve` returns `source: 'unassigned'` + `agentSlug: null` on a miss and the resolver raises `AGENT_NOT_ASSIGNED { task, tenantId, departmentId }` (503 on the internal route, DEGRADED in the harness); `PromptResolutionService` raises `PROMPT_DEFAULT_NOT_PROVISIONED`; both name the re-sync route. | A silent `null` that falls through to legacy code paths — indistinguishable from "no workflow"; a generic 500. |
| P-31 | **(round 3)** L14 runs in wave 2b (five lanes, the cap) with the three producer lines HANDED to L3, rather than in wave 3. | Wave 3 — waits a wave for three one-liners and collides with L13 on `prompt-management.service.ts`. |
| P-32 | **(D-1 answered YES, 2026-09-06)** The opt-out extends to the mandatory clinical guard nodes by REMOVING ONE FILTER, not by rewriting the safety model: `withRuntimeProperties` (`node-config-schemas.ts:2252`) stops gating the `enabled` fold on `MANDATORY_NODE_TYPES` and gates it on a new two-member `GRAPH_BOUNDARY_NODE_TYPES` (`core.trigger`, `core.output`) instead; the set itself and its registry mirror stay; `rule-catalogue.ts`, `port-validation.ts`'s `requires` check and both runtimes need NO edit (none of them reads `config.enabled`), so mandatory PRESENCE and guard ATTACHMENT keep holding while EXECUTION becomes optional; the compensating controls are a `GUARDRAIL_OPTED_OUT` publish WARNING naming every disabled mandatory node, per-call `attributesJson.guardrail: 'opted_out'` with the disabled node ids, and the existing `SKIPPED(disabled_by_config)` step. `consultation.hitlGate` gains nothing (it is excluded from the runtime fold already), so TASK-859 invariant 5 is untouched. §3.14a. | Deleting members from `MANDATORY_NODE_TYPES` — that would relax PRESENCE too, letting a tenant publish a consultation graph with no consent gate at all, and would silently change what `REQUIRED_PATH_THROUGH` enforces. Making the toggle a separate `guardrail.enabled` key on each clinical node — a second vocabulary for the fact `config.enabled` already expresses, with two runtimes to teach. Giving `consultation.hitlGate` a disable toggle — needs `compileGate` to honour it and is a decision about who signs, not about which guard runs. |
| P-33 | **(D-2 answered YES, 2026-09-06)** `text.externalGuardrail.enabled` is seeded ON as ONE create-only SYSTEM `GlobalSetting` row in `seed/11-global-setting.ts`, owned by L14 in wave 2b, gated by proof #8 (the live value on dev / test / k3s) and by an orchestrator-run stack precondition: every stack the e2e suite runs against must have a reachable guardrail client, or every generation 503s by design. The descriptor is untouched (`default: false`, `killSwitch: true`), so the platform can still turn the gate off without a deploy. §3.14b. | Changing the descriptor default — makes the kill switch un-turn-off-able-by-absence and hides the flip from the settings registry. Leaving it OFF — the opt-out toggles an inert gate and the first production tenant runs unmoderated until someone remembers the key. Seeding it ON without the stack check — every generation in the isolated test stack 503s and the e2e suite goes red for a non-bug. |

| OD-O | Retire the per-tenant catalogue clone and soft-delete today's clone rows? | Yes. | For the catalogue: refer to the SYSTEM catalogue for built-in Hope providers-models, no clone or copy; BYO providers-models are tenant-specific. | Decided: L13 deletes `provisionTenantModelCatalog` + backfill after proof #3; `AiModel` stays shared-read (config); the BYO slug guard added (P-29). |
| OD-P | Retire `provisionTenantConfigs` (Global-tenant clone) or repoint to SYSTEM? | Retire behind proof #7. | Following the suggestion, AND only the platform admin may manage the platform settings; they are global-wide effective. | Retire (L13); the legacy CRUD guard (§3.15, L1, P-28); "global-wide effective" already true via `AppSettingsService`. |
| OD-Q | Confirm "Hope provider" = every SYSTEM row incl. platform-keyed cloud rows. | Yes. | Yes — the out-of-the-box provider-model set including cloud providers, set as default, cross-tenant, in the SYSTEM tenant. | L1's DTO shape unblocked (wave 1). |
| OD-R | Confirm the reference-set kinds; guardrail policy NOT cloned. | Yes. | Guardrail is platform-wide, shared, platform-admin-managed, not cloned — AND the tenant admin MUST be able to opt out per agent node or workflow. | Kinds confirmed; §3.14 opt-out design; L14 added (wave 2b); P-27; D-1 / D-2 opened as §6.3 discussion items and **both answered YES on 2026-09-06** — §3.14a (mandatory clinical guard nodes in scope, P-32) and §3.14b (the switch seeded ON, P-33). |

### 6.3 Discussion items — answered (2026-09-06)

| # | Item | Owner answer (verbatim) | Plan consequence | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | Does the OD-R opt-out extend to the MANDATORY clinical guard nodes? | **"D-1: does the opt-out extend to the seven mandatory clinical guard nodes? YES."** | The tenant-admin opt-out ALSO covers the node types in `MANDATORY_NODE_TYPES` (`node-config-schemas.ts:2226-2247`): `enabled` stops being withheld from them, so `guardrail.check`, `consultation.consentGate`, `consultation.captureBinding`, `consultation.phiHop`, `consultation.persistDraft`, `consultation.finalizeAssurance` and the summarization / STT mandatory types all offer the key. Mandatory PRESENCE is unchanged — the consultation and summarization rule sets read the GRAPH, so a present-but-disabled node still satisfies every `NODE_TYPE_PRESENT` / `REQUIRED_PATH_THROUGH` rule, and publish still refuses a graph in which the node was removed; `requires: ['guard.groundedness']` (`node-registry.ts:1267,1286,1305`) is likewise satisfied by a present-but-disabled guard, because `port-validation.ts:302-321` checks neighbour TYPES. Execution needs no new code (`SKIPPED(disabled_by_config)` already exists on both lanes). The compensating controls: `GUARDRAIL_OPTED_OUT` (WARNING) names every disabled mandatory node at publish, and the run / ledger record `guardrail: 'opted_out'` with the disabled node ids. The two in-code statements that call this "a compliance defect" (`node-config-schemas.ts:2211-2214`, `:2172-2176`) are rewritten to RECORD the owner decision. Two things do NOT move: `consultation.hitlGate` (already outside the runtime fold via `RUNTIME_PROPERTY_EXCLUSIONS:2202`) so **TASK-859 invariant 5 "the system never signs" is untouched**, and the two graph boundaries `core.trigger` / `core.output`, whose disable would be an unrunnable graph rather than a guardrail opinion. | §3.14 boundary row, §3.14a (seven change points), P-32, L14 wave 2b; §4.5 `task-890-guardrail-optout.spec.ts`; Risk 21 |
| D-2 | Seed the external-guardrail kill switch ON? | **"D-2: seed the external-guardrail switch on? YES."** | `text.externalGuardrail.enabled` is seeded ON as ONE create-only SYSTEM `GlobalSetting` row in `packages/database/src/prisma/db_main/seed/11-global-setting.ts` (L14 owns the file, wave 2b). The descriptor is untouched (`maxScope: 'system'`, `globalOnly: true`, `killSwitch: true`, `default: false`), so a platform admin can still turn the gate off without a deploy. Two preconditions, both the ORCHESTRATOR's: proof #8 (the live value on dev, the test DB and the k3s `hope-v2-dev` DB) runs first, and every stack the e2e suite runs against must have a reachable guardrail client — `pnpm stack:dev` and the k3s dev namespace already do; the isolated test stack (`tests/docker-compose.test.yml` + `pnpm setup:test` / `pnpm test:up:api`) does not, so it gains one or the e2e run points TEXT at a running guardrail. Without that, every generation 503s by design (`generate.py:207-213`, `output_gate.py:202-204`). Latency / cost delta recorded in §9 after the first e2e run with it on. | §3.14b, P-33, L14 wave 2b; proof #8 (§4.4); §4.7 step 5 / §4.9 wave-2b close; Risk 22 |

### 6.4 Open owner questions at close (2026-09-07)

Twenty-four questions: **Q-1 … Q-9** from the four implementation waves, **Q-10 … Q-24** from the
black-box release phase that followed them (§9). None blocks what shipped; each carries the
recommendation already given, so an owner answer is a yes/no, not a design round.

| # | Question | Where it came from | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q-1 | **Mandatory node PRESENCE is advisory at publish.** Deleting `consultation.consentGate` publishes **200** with `validationReport.ok: false` (`WF-CONS-001`), because DRAFT rule-catalogue findings never block the transition (design decision #3, pre-dating this ticket). D-1's compensating claim — "may be switched OFF, never REMOVED" — is therefore true of the recorded finding, not of the refusal. | wave-2b close; pinned honestly by `task-890-guardrail-optout.spec.ts` (the test asserts what HAPPENS and takes the stronger branch automatically if this is ever fixed) | Promote the consultation core rule set out of DRAFT, **or** move mandatory-presence into `publishFindings` as a blocking code. TASK-904 is written and waiting on this answer |
| Q-2 | **`svc:admin:agent:manage` is missing from the seeded service account** (`seed/94-service-account.ts`), so the service-account credential class is unproven for all 21 `admin/agents` routes and §4.5's draft-bench SA depth case is skipped. | wave-2b close | Add it — the scope is derived and real (`admin:agent:manage` → `manage:Agent`, held by TENANT_ADMIN). It is a one-line seed insertion, but widening a seeded credential is an owner call. TASK-907 |
| Q-3 | **Tenant-config key addressing** — `PATCH admin/tenants/configs/:key` addresses a SYSTEM row by a key that is not tenant-qualified. Wave 1 closed the privilege hole; the ADDRESSING is still ambiguous. | wave-1 close | Leave as is for now; revisit with the settings-registry lane (TASK-870's successor). No exploitable path remains |
| Q-4 | **A reserved-tenant entitlement override is a no-op.** Writing an override for a `RESERVED_UNGATED` tenant succeeds and changes nothing, because enforcement is skipped for those tenants entirely. | wave-1 close | Refuse the write with a named 409 rather than accept one that cannot take effect |
| Q-5 | **`wireModelId` has no Postgres CHECK.** It is conditionally NOT NULL for CLOUD rows in code only; a direct SQL insert can leave a cloud row unroutable. | wave-2a close | Add the CHECK constraint with TASK-899 (`AiModel.provider` as an enum) — one migration, two constraints |
| Q-6 | **D-1 presence is ADVISORY** — same fact as Q-1, stated as the owner's own compensating control. Recorded here because §3.14a still reads as though presence were enforced. | wave-2b close | Answer Q-1; §3.14a's wording follows from it |
| Q-7 | **Azure `deploymentName` is optional on a BYO model declaration.** A tenant can declare an Azure model with no deployment name, which the wire repoint then cannot route. | wave-2b close | Make it required for `provider: 'azure'` at declaration time (one DTO rule), rather than failing at run time |
| Q-8 | **`DEPARTMENT_ENDPOINTS.PROMPT_CONFIG` had no gateway route.** The wave-3 close removed the SDK's dead read; the DEF-C5 department config tier is therefore absent, not broken. | wave-3 close | Add a self-scoped `GET users/me/department/prompt-config` (the caller's own department, no admin ability) and restore tier 2 against it. TASK-908 |
| Q-9 | **Two platform workflow templates can never be provisioned** — `platform-consultation-grammar-fix` and `platform-consultation-ner-grammar-fix` pin PLATFORM-owned prompt/document catalogue rows, so `assertNoUnresolvableCatalogBindings` refuses the clone (correctly: the copy would carry references the tenant cannot resolve). Every tenant is therefore provisioned WITHOUT them, and the reference-set summary reports it as a stated skip rather than a failure. | wave-3 close (L13) | Re-point the bindings at the target tenant's own clones by `sourceTemplateId` — exactly what `AgentService.referenceInstruction` already does for an agent's prompt — and refuse only when no clone exists. TASK-909 |
| Q-10 | **The platform-default election has no `ModelTaskType` → `AiTaskKind` matrix.** Electing a text-generation model as the platform default for `EMBEDDING` is accepted — nothing checks that the model's task type can serve the task kind | black-box J1 (MAJOR-B) | Declare the matrix once and check it at election time; the pairs are already implicit in `AGENT_TASK_MODEL_TASK_TYPE` |
| Q-11 | **What is the end state for prompt scoping — tags only?** Prompts now carry tags end to end and the console filters on them, which is what makes `dept:<code>` scoping work while departments are retired. `PromptTemplate.departmentId` still exists and still resolves. Does the column go, and when? | black-box J2 | Land the tag convention (`dept:<Department.code lowercased>`, `visit:<key>`, subset/AND — the agent tag grammar), backfill from `departmentId` and the Department pointer columns, then drop the column in the release after |
| Q-12 | **Should seed phase 26 clone `WorkflowDefinition` rows into a tenant?** The allow-list comment claimed a seed clone path that does not exist (fixed as a comment); whether the SEED should do what the reference-set service does is undecided | black-box J7-3 | Leave the seed alone — `TenantReferenceSetService` is the one copier, and the seed calls it |
| Q-13 | **`WORKFLOW_EXPOSURE_ENABLED` is an `env`-tier flag.** It now defaults ON, but a flag that must change without a restart is not an env var (rule 09 §Configuration Tiers) | black-box J7-5 | Move it to `redis-flag` with the rest of the kill switches |
| Q-14 | **Eighteen legacy per-tenant `GlobalSetting` keys have no SYSTEM tier**, so OD-P's platform-tier reasoning finds nothing to protect for them | black-box J7-4 | Either retire them with the TASK-865 block or give each a SYSTEM row at `seed/11-global-setting.ts:440` |
| Q-15 | **Should the consultation plane resolve a workflow by trigger KIND rather than by palette?** Today only a `consultation`-palette row can govern a consultation, so a `core`-palette workflow declaring `kinds: [consultation]` — assigned at tenant AND department — is reachable only through `POST /workflows/{slug}/runs` | black-box J5-F4 | Resolve by declared trigger kind, with the palette as a tie-break; TASK-916 is written and waiting on this |
| Q-16 | **May an API key mint a run-scoped stream ticket?** `POST /auth/stream-ticket` is `@ForbidApiKey()`, so the `socket` protocol a published workflow advertises is unreachable for the developer the publish is FOR | black-box J6 | Yes, scoped to one run id and one short TTL — that keeps the admin-plane rule intact while making the advertised protocol true. TASK-914 |
| Q-17 | **Is the FLAT trigger payload the authoring shape?** `context.*` in an agent prompt resolves against the run payload ROOT while `core.classify` reads `text` off the same root, so a `{text, context:{…}}` payload satisfies neither. J6's workflow declares them flat and works | black-box J6 | Yes — and warn at publish when a trigger schema nests a kind under a namespace an agent node then reads flat |
| Q-18 | **Should `blocking` be withdrawn from a graph that carries a durable human gate?** It is advertised, and it can only ever reach the gateway's 60 s ceiling | black-box J6 | Withdraw it at publish, in `modesFor`. TASK-919 |

The realtime path on the same audio was clean | black-box J5-F5 | Either honour `language=en` by narrowing the resolved spec, or elect a second ASR agent for English-only work |
| Q-20 | **What threshold should `response_toxicity` carry?** The SYSTEM `TenantGuardrailPolicy` entry is `{enabled: true}` with none, so the classifier's default blocks a 945-token clinical JSON completion as toxic | black-box F14 | Give the policy entry a threshold and tune it against clinical output; the per-node opt-out is the tenant-side mitigation, not the fix. TASK-913 |
| Q-21 | **Should `resultRef` carry the successful nodes' payload on a DEGRADED run?** It is null today even when every node before the degraded one succeeded | black-box F13/F14 | Carry it, and let the run status say the run degraded. TASK-920 |
| Q-22 | **Why does the f16 whisper row read `unknown` while the Q8_0 row from the SAME repo reads ready?** `arcaai-whisper-large-ml-en-gguf` never came back from a readiness sweep that answered for its sibling | black-box F6 / cache move | Probably a per-file rather than per-repo resolution; worth one look before the readiness snapshot is trusted for capacity decisions |
| Q-23 | **`.env.test:1600` still points `HF_HOME` at the external volume.** Only the test API reads it, and that stack does not run models — but it is the same wedge that took two services down in dev | black-box cache move | Point it at the same local cache, or at nothing |
| Q-24 | **There is no rollback / re-activate endpoint for an already-published agent version.** Returning to v1 after publishing v2 means deprecate + branch + publish, which mints a NEW version number rather than re-activating the one that was fine | black-box J3 | Add an explicit re-activate on a published version. TASK-925 |

## 7. Risks and mitigations

| # | Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Fail-closed templating makes existing content unpublishable on its next publish (OD-C). | WARNING in release 1 with the proof #5 census and a console banner per tenant; ERROR in release +1 (P-4). |
| 2 | Deleting the `{var}` grammar touches the live consultation summary path (six `PromptAssemblyService` callers). | Seeds converted in the same wave; golden snapshots of the assembled prompts before/after are byte-identical (L3 step 1); proof #4 asserts no single-brace row survives the seed; fixture case #16 pins "single brace is literal". |
| 3 | Wiring the publish gate makes currently-published graphs unpublishable. | Proof #1 before L0 merges; non-empty = a data-migration task; release-1 enforcement scoped to `core.*` (P-8). |
| 4 | 62 of the registered node types are deprecated and the seed authoring sources are still legacy (TASK-864 §7 B2). | The `core.*` scope in release 1; the palette removal is TASK-901 (OD-N). |
| 5 | The realtime/durable fix CHANGES output for agents already using dotted paths on the realtime lane. | Proof #2 lists them before L3 merges; the change is expected, not a regression. |
| 6 | `AiModel.provider` is nullable free text; a picker keyed on it hides mis-labelled rows. | Hidden from tenants, counted for the super admin (proof #3, the badge); an enum / check constraint is TASK-899. |
| 7 | Five-artifact regeneration is a merge-conflict magnet (eight lanes add or change routes). | No lane commits artifacts; the orchestrator regenerates once per merge and runs the three `:check`s (TASK-805's pipeline #990 precedent). |
| 8 | `read:AiModel` is a new ability: a route no seeded role holds is unreachable, and a route with no decorator refuses boot; `tenant-admin-authority.test.ts` pins the pair. | L1 lands the controller, the scope-registry entry, the seed rule and the authority test together, and pastes the boot line. |
| 9 | `Agent` / `AiModel` / `PromptTemplate` / `WorkflowDefinition` are all touched by ONE migration while TASK-885 is open. | L2 rebases on the `dev-2.2` head immediately before authoring; shadow-DB only; the empty-diff line is pasted; L1's "no reader remains" grep is pasted before the column drop. |
| 10 | Scope inflation (13 lanes, 11 packages). | §4.6 day-1 cut = the seven BLOCKERs; the fan-out cap of five per wave; L9/L10/L12 are the deferrable full-cut lanes. |
| 11 | The compiled-config snapshot has TWO parity gates (`compiled-config.schema.json` with `additionalProperties: false`; pydantic `extra="forbid"`). | One lane (L2) edits both sides with defaults, so an artifact compiled before the field parses (the `documentTemplateRefs` precedent). |
| 12 | Service-account scopes are raw strings; an unregistered `svc:admin:ai-model:read` either passes silently or trips the surface audit. | L1 registers it (implied by `:manage`) and the boot smoke proves the audit accepts it. |
| 13 | **Retiring the catalogue clone changes what post-TASK-860 tenants see** (their private `AiModel` copies vanish; agents bound to a CLONE row keep an FK to a soft-deleted row and fail their next publish with `MODEL_DISABLED`). | Proof #3 lists tenants + counts + agents bound to clone rows BEFORE the backfill; the backfill script re-binds such agents to the SYSTEM row of the same slug (same `resolveModelIdBySlugVisible` rule) in the same transaction and reports each; OD-O is asked explicitly. |
| 14 | **Removing the admin hooks from `@arcaai/vox`** breaks an EXTERNAL consumer this repo cannot see (the published npm package). | §2.7 #2 proves zero first-party consumers; the 2.1 changelog names `hope.admin.*` / the console as the replacement; every `/admin/*` route is `@ForbidApiKey()`, so no API-key integration could ever have used them. |
| 15 | **Metering a previously-free route changes tenant cost**: invocations, the realtime lane and test-runs start writing `AiUsageEvent` rows and, once a plan sets a ceiling, can 429. | Every seeded allowance is `null` (unlimited) today, so the prechecks are no-ops until a platform admin sets a ceiling; the rows are the visible change; the console test panels say "counts against your allowance"; `trigger` lets finance separate tests from production. |
| 16 | **Readiness staleness** vs "at that point of time": a 30 s snapshot can show `ready` for an engine that just died. | The DTO carries `readinessCheckedAt`; publish treats readiness as advisory; run time fails closed on the real 503 (`connection.py:55-70`); the platform admin has "Probe now". |
| 17 | The `wireModelId` NOT NULL backfill mislabels an engine-served row whose `sourceUri` is not its engine id (the seeds set both equal; a DB row may differ). | Proof #3 adds `SELECT slug, "sourceUri", "wireModelId" FROM core."AiModel" WHERE "wireModelId" IS NOT NULL AND "wireModelId" <> "sourceUri"` before L2; any row is resolved by hand. |
| 18 | A tenant created between the L2 merge and the L13 backfill has no `consultation-legacy-v1` clone; `PromptAssemblyService` would 503. | L3 keeps the code-owned name list as a fallback until L13 deletes it in the same wave the backfill runs; proof #6 after the backfill. |
| 19 | The Azure `deployment_name` precedence change silently reroutes a SYSTEM-row request. | The omission applies ONLY to candidates whose model row carries `sourceConnectionId`; the `apps/text` contract test pins both branches; the SYSTEM Azure row's behaviour is byte-identical. |
| 20 | **(round 3) Flipping the shared-read set before the backfill** — an explicit-`tenantId` read crashes (`AgentAssignmentRepository.findAllForScope`), an implicit one goes silent (`AgentRepository` visible family, `PromptTemplateRepository.findById`), and an unprovisioned tenant loses its platform agents / prompts with no error until a consultation fails (§2.7 #22). | The flip is L13 step v, a SEPARATE merge after steps i–iv, gated by proof #9 = 0 on dev AND test and by the FULL e2e; every reader is rewritten to explicit tenant-only + named errors in step iii, so a miss after the flip is `AGENT_NOT_ASSIGNED` / `PROMPT_DEFAULT_NOT_PROVISIONED`, never a crash or a silence; the two e2e specs that assume the cascade become regression pins on the provisioned test tenants. |
| 21 | **(round 3, widened by D-1) Opt-out misuse in clinical graphs** — a tenant admin switches guardrail off on a `core.agent` node that writes clinical documentation, or (after D-1) disables a mandatory clinical guard node such as `consultation.consentGate`, `consultation.phiHop` or `guardrail.check`. The risk is REAL and stays: the owner has accepted it deliberately (D-1, §6.3), so the mitigation is attribution, not prevention. | **What still cannot happen:** the node cannot be REMOVED (mandatory presence is unchanged — §3.14a #4), the human sign-off `consultation.hitlGate` gains no toggle (invariant 5 untouched), the graph boundaries stay un-disableable, a tenant can only turn the gate OFF and never ON over a platform kill switch, and there is still no `fail_open`. **What makes misuse visible:** every disabled mandatory node is named by id + type + registry classes in a `GUARDRAIL_OPTED_OUT` publish WARNING that lands on `validationReport` and renders in the Studio rail and the agent findings; every affected call carries `attributesJson.guardrail: 'opted_out'` with the disabled node ids, so "which tenants ran without a consent gate, and how often" is one ledger query; the run carries a `SKIPPED(disabled_by_config)` step; and the publish itself is an audited `ResourceUpdated` with the full graph diff. Every opted-out node is a `GUARDRAIL_OPTED_OUT` publish WARNING visible in the Studio rail and the agent findings; the decision is RECORDED per call (`attributesJson.guardrail`, the step result, the TEXT usage reason) so finance and safety can list every opted-out invocation; the platform switch remains the floor (a tenant can only turn OFF). |
| 22 | **(round 3, DECIDED by D-2 = YES) Turning the external-guardrail gate ON changes latency and cost** for every generation on every tenant — one guardrail round-trip in, one delegated `classify` + PII-span call out — **and 503s every generation wherever TEXT has no reachable guardrail client** (fail-closed by design, `generate.py:207-213`, `output_gate.py:202-204`). The sharpest edge is the isolated test stack: `tests/docker-compose.test.yml` / `pnpm setup:test` start data infrastructure only, so seeding the row without fixing that turns the whole e2e suite red for a non-bug. | Ordered, and owned by the ORCHESTRATOR (§3.14b, §4.9 wave-2b close): proof #8 on dev / test / k3s FIRST; then the stack precondition — a reachable guardrail client on every environment where TEXT runs, the test stack included — confirmed BEFORE the seed row merges and before wave 2b's e2e; the row is create-only, so it never reverts a deliberate flip; the key keeps `killSwitch: true` so a platform admin can turn it back off without a deploy; if the stack cannot be fixed inside the wave the row is HELD as a one-row follow-up and the opt-out ships regardless; the latency / cost delta is measured on the first e2e run with it on and recorded in §9. |
| 23 | **(round 3) The legacy settings guard changes an error code** — a tenant admin who today gets a 400 (interceptor) or a raw 500 (extension) on a SYSTEM row gets a 403. | No first-party caller depends on the old codes (the console renders the row read-only from the 403 or was never able to reach it); the e2e depth spec pins the new contract; the registry lane is untouched. |

Four close commits on top: `83a84f11c` (the L1↔L12 integration + the five artifacts), `5813fec3f`
(three defects fixed), `f63f021d7` (the lifecycle fixture), plus the rule-05 row `cb6853ea8` the
orchestrator landed with L1.

**Integration fix — one readiness port, one symbol, one type set.** L1 and L12 each declared a
readiness vocabulary and an `IInferenceReadinessService` symbol in their own worktree; merged, that
was four TS2308 ambiguous re-exports and a `@arcaai/applications` that did not build. Resolved in
favour of the lane that MEASURES readiness: `services/ai-readiness` owns `ModelReadiness`,
`InferenceReadinessSnapshot`, the symbol and `modelReadinessFrom`; `ai-model/model-readiness.port.ts`
is deleted; the catalogue DTO re-exports the canonical union instead of restating it;
`AiModelService` injects the same symbol narrowed to `Pick<…,'getSnapshot'>` so a catalogue read can
never trigger a probe. `ReadinessProviderClass` is now an alias of the provider plane's
`ProviderClass`, and the interim `ENGINE_SERVED_PROVIDERS` / `PLATFORM_SELF_HOST_SENTINEL` copies are
gone — both come from `ai-provider-connection/constants.ts`, beside `providerClassOf`.
`AiModelServiceModule` imports `InferenceReadinessServiceModule`, so the catalogue actually stamps
`modelReadinessFrom(snapshot, model.id)` (`unknown`, with no timestamp, on a cold snapshot); the
silent-optional hazard is pinned by the module test next to the provider-connection assertion.

**Defects found reviewing the merged tree, and fixed here** (each verified against the running stack
before it was touched):

| # | Where | Defect | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| W1-1 | `tenant.service.ts:1149` | `PATCH admin/tenants/configs/__SYSTEM__` walked around §3.15's platform-tier guard: the controller's scope check only 404s a UUID identifier, so a KEY skips it; `GlobalSetting` is SYSTEM-shared-READ, so a tenant admin's `findById` resolves a platform row; the ownership check compares the row to the NAMED tenant, not the caller; the write goes through the unscoped base client. `locked` stopped 5 of the 37 seeded SYSTEM rows; nothing stopped the other 32 | the same 403 the sibling guard raises, on the SYSTEM tier, with the cross-tenant 404 untouched; two tests pin both directions |
| W1-2 | `inference-readiness.service.ts:400` | a model's `readinessDetail` forwarded TEXT's raw `probe_error` (`str(exc)`) to any holder of `read:AiModel`; for an httpx failure that string carries the engine's internal URL — the very thing the sweep's `hostOf()` reduction exists to strip | the MODEL verdict is a curated phrase; the ENGINE entry keeps the diagnostic and that document is super-admin-only |
| W1-3 | `ai-service-admin.controller.ts` | `POST admin/ai-services/readiness/refresh` answered 201 while its own `@ApiOkResponse` and the published `openapi.json` said 200 | `@HttpCode(HttpStatus.OK)`; artifacts regenerated |
| W1-4 | `task-890-metering.spec.ts` | the 429 case was asserted against `__GLOBAL__`, which is in `RESERVED_UNGATED_TENANT_IDS`: with no plan it resolves `UNGATED_ENTITLEMENTS` and Q3 IGNORES the override, so `assertMeterQuota` returned on `limit === null` and the call reached TEXT (503). The one assertion that would have proven the precheck proved nothing | stamps a plan for the length of the describe (`effectivePlan` rule 1 — the documented escape hatch), restores it, and skips with a named reason if the tenant still resolves ungated. Now PASSES: 429, `DOMAIN.QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, no ledger row |
| W1-5 | `task-890-metering.spec.ts` | the `PROMPT_TEST` case asserts L4's emitter, which is wave 2b — a red that would only say "the wave that owns it has not run" | `test.fixme` naming the lane; the body needs no change when L4 lands |
| W1-6 | `task-779-workflow-lifecycle.spec.ts` | four tests went red on the newly-wired publish gate. The findings were CORRECT: the fixture chained all eleven canonical types on the DATA ports (type-incompatible at `bindTerminology[entities] → phiHop[transcript]` and `sensors[verdict] → persistDraft[document]`) and stamped `emitsTrajectory` on the two graph boundaries, whose schemas are `additionalProperties: false` | the chain moves to the CONTROL pair (`next` → `after`) and boundaries take their own config only. 11 passed; the WF-CONS-007 test still publishes, so rule findings stay non-blocking and the two concepts remain distinct |

are asserted unconditionally — and `task-890-readiness.spec.ts`'s catalogue-stamping case is

Funding itself is still DERIVED (`fundingTier === 'tenant'`), so inv-3 holds; this is a derivation edge, and which way it should fall is a billing decision | `agent.controller.ts:456-461`; `vocabulary.ts:131-134` |
